### Profiles, identity, and privacy

| ID      | Status      | Size | Feature                           | User-facing behavior                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Dependencies                                            |
| ------- | ----------- | ---- | --------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| PROF-03 | `SHIPPED`   | L    | Unique username                   | Every account has a normalized unique handle with shared format and reserved-name rules. Settings edits it, search accepts names or `@username` without exposing email, and authenticated profile URLs use the handle while old UUID links remain valid.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Shipped 2026-09-09                                      |
| PROF-04 | `SHIPPED`   | L    | Real biography and location       | Settings edits an optional biography (500 characters) and location (100 characters). Blank values normalize to empty state, each field has its own private-by-default visibility, and profiles distinguish real, empty and private content without querying denied fields. Shipped 2026-09-09 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.15.0`, a required BE migration and FE settings/profile views.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | PROF-06; `@sunsteel/contracts@0.15.0`                   |
| PROF-05 | `SHIPPED`   | L    | Training identity                 | Settings manages optional broad goals (up to 3), experience, disciplines (up to 3), preferred routine style and catalog-backed favorite exercises (up to 5). Profiles render honest empty/private states or the structured identity allowed by one private-by-default visibility rule. These labels describe training identity; measurable targets remain with `PROG-08`. Shipped 2026-09-10 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.16.0`, a required BE migration and FE settings/profile views.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | PROF-06; `@sunsteel/contracts@0.16.0`                   |
| PROF-06 | `SHIPPED`   | XL   | Privacy model                     | Settings controls `public`, `followers`, or `private` visibility independently for biography, location, training identity, workout history, records, routines, achievements, and body metrics. All eight default to private; email is never part of the member-profile response. The authenticated profile read enforces owner/follower/public access before querying sensitive profile, training or body data, and currently renders allowed About content, training identity, training summaries, records and body metrics. Routine and achievement rules are persisted for their future profile surfaces. The five-domain foundation shipped 2026-09-09 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.14.0`; `PROF-04` and `PROF-05` extended it through `0.16.0`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | BE/CT authorization rules; `@sunsteel/contracts@0.16.0` |
| PROF-07 | `SHIPPED`   | L    | Featured records and achievements | Owners choose and order up to six typed accomplishments in Settings and the resolved ledger appears on authenticated and public profiles. The persisted slots and shared payload accept current records, earned achievements and one attained rank; ACH-03 now supplies all three choices through that foundation. Records follow records privacy; achievement/rank slots follow achievements privacy; stale, denied or no-longer-earned references are omitted at read time. Shipped 2026-09-16 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.44.0` and migration `20260916090000_featured_profile_items`. Evaluation: [PROF-07 scope](../history/prof07-evaluation-2026-09-15.md); closure: [PROF-07 closure](../history/prof07-closure-2026-09-16.md).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | DATA-01, ACH-01, PROF-06                                |
| PROF-08 | `SHIPPED`   | M    | Routine showcase                  | `FeaturedProfileItemKind` gained `ROUTINE`, so a shared routine is a fourth typed slot in the same ordered six the shipped `PROF-07` model already stores, revalidates and privacy-filters — not a second curation surface in Settings nor a second section on the profile. The resolved payload is the `ROUT-04` `SharedRoutineSummary`, read through one shared select and mapper so a featured routine and a listed one cannot disagree. **The rule is asked twice, deliberately differently:** selecting, Settings offers only routines whose effective visibility is not `PRIVATE`, because featuring one nobody can open would show its owner a slot that never renders; reading, `canViewRoutine` decides, so the `PROF-06` routines rule still caps each routine's own setting and a denied slot is omitted rather than emptied. A featured routine opens `/routines/<id>` for its owner, `/profile/<identifier>/routines/<routineId>` for another member, and nothing signed out. Shipped 2026-09-18 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.50.0` and migration `20260918090000_featured_routine_kind`. | ROUT-04, PROF-06, PROF-07                               |
| PROF-09 | `SHIPPED`   | L    | Account discovery controls        | Settings independently controls discovery by first/last name, public `@username`, and future contact matching. Name and username remain enabled by default to preserve existing search; contact matching is explicit opt-in and is not active today. Search enforces each current control without affecting direct profile URLs or section privacy. Shipped 2026-09-10 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.17.0`, a required BE migration and FE settings card.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | PROF-03, PROF-06; `@sunsteel/contracts@0.17.0`          |
| PROF-10 | `SHIPPED`   | L    | Block and report                  | A block is **symmetric and enforced by the server before every read**: it removes any follow in both directions, prevents a new one either way, and hides each account from the other's search, suggestions, relationship lists, profile and routines. A blocked profile answers **404, never 403**, exactly as a denied routine does under `ROUT-04` -- a 403 would confirm both that the account exists and that a block is the reason. It is deliberately **not retroactive over tokens already handed out**: a `SOC-07` or `ROUT-04` link carries no viewer identity, so revoking the link stays the control for that, and the copy says so rather than implying otherwise. A report is **recorded and honestly acknowledged**, with a fixed reason list rather than free text; the queue, the review actions and the enforcement record are `TRUST-04`, and nothing here promises a human will read it. Shipped 2026-09-18 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.51.0` and migration `20260918140000_blocks_reports_lineage`.                                                                              | BE/CT moderation model                                  |
| PROF-11 | `SHIPPED`     | M    | Branded profile card              | Shipped 2026-09-23. Every owner, authenticated-member and signed-out public profile can generate a 1200×1500 branded PNG from exactly the identity, current Renaissance rank and featured accomplishments that profile is already allowed to show. The image reuses the rank's shipped crest family and pigment, keeps an honest empty state when no accomplishment is selected, uses the native file-share sheet where the browser supports it and otherwise downloads a predictably named PNG. Generation stays client-side over existing privacy-filtered data: no new read, stored derivative, contract or backend path.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | PROF-03, PROF-07, PROF-12                               |
| PROF-12 | `SHIPPED`   | L    | Public profile route and sharing  | `/members/<username>` sits outside the protected shell and reads an unguarded, no-store backend endpoint that evaluates every section as a signed-out visitor: only `PUBLIC` data is queried and returned, while `FOLLOWERS` and `PRIVATE` remain hidden. Authenticated `/profile/<identifier>` retains owner/follower access, UUID compatibility and social controls. Share Profile copies the public URL with explicit success or failure feedback. Shipped 2026-09-10 without a contract publish or database migration.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | PROF-03, PROF-06                                        |

### Social and community

| ID     | Status        | Size | Feature                    | User-facing behavior                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Dependencies                                           |
| ------ | ------------- | ---- | -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| SOC-02 | `SHIPPED`     | M    | Relationship lists         | Signed-in members open any profile's followers, following and viewer-relative mutuals (people you follow who also follow them; mutual follows on your own profile) from the profile counts, paginated newest first with Follow/Unfollow on every row. Search suggests up to ten members: people who already follow you first, then accounts followed by people you follow that allow name or username discovery. Entries carry identity only, never email or privacy-governed sections, and the signed-out `/members` route keeps plain counts. Shipped 2026-09-13 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.24.0` without a migration.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | FIX-06; `@sunsteel/contracts@0.24.0`                   |
| SOC-03 | `SHIPPED`     | L    | Generated activity feed    | A feed built from what members actually did, not from posts they wrote, with no free-text composer. **Seven activity types, each one fact the product already verifies:** completed sessions, personal records, load progressions, earned achievements, streak milestones, `ACH-05` comebacks and `ROUT-04` shared routines. They are **generated on read and never stored** -- from `TrainingEvent` rows, the shipped `comebackRecognitionSummary` with its own bound, and a routine's new `sharedAt`, set when a private routine first becomes visible -- so nothing can drift from the data it describes. Achievements recognized from history never produce an entry (they have no honest date, as `NOTIF-01` decided), and a streak is reported once, as the streak, although it is written twice. **Rank progression is not a type:** nothing records when a rank was reached, and deriving it would replay a member's whole history on every read; challenges wait on `SOC-13`. `/activity` shows the members the viewer follows, and another member's profile shows their activity to whoever its audience allows; the facts of one workout are shown together. Each entry names the record it came from and **links to it only where the viewer may open it**, a decision the server makes: a record only while it is still the best the profile shows, and a session or load progression, which no page shows another member, never. Activity is signed-in only. Shipped 2026-09-20 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.53.0` and migration `20260919120000_activity_sharing`. | DATA-02, PROF-06, PROF-10                              |
| SOC-04 | `SHIPPED`     | M    | Selective activity sharing | A default audience per activity type -- Only me, Followers or Everyone, the `PROF-06` values -- **starting at Only me**, so nothing reaches anyone until its owner chooses; a default applies to past entries of its type too, and the control says so. Any single entry can then be given its own audience or withdrawn, which is the same thing as choosing Only me for it. **Profile privacy is the upper bound through the shipped rule:** each type maps to one section, and an entry is visible only when `canViewProfileSection` allows both that section and the entry's audience -- the shape `canViewRoutine` has -- so a private records section cannot produce a public record entry, not even through an override; a shared routine must also pass `canViewRoutine`. The owner is told which section, or which routine, is capping a choice. **The preview runs the same read as a hypothetical follower or non-follower**, so it shows exactly what that audience receives rather than a second calculation of it. Everyone means every signed-in member; activity never appears on the signed-out `/members` page. Shipped 2026-09-20 with `SOC-03`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | SOC-03, PROF-06                                        |
| SOC-05 | `SHIPPED`     | M    | Themed reactions           | Four themed reactions -- Strength, Discipline, Respect and Inspiring -- acknowledge one generated activity entry, in the feed and on a member's profile. **The activity read is the only gate**: the summary travels inside the entry it belongs to, so a reaction can never be shown on an entry the viewer was not allowed to see, and accepting one resolves the entry's author, rebuilds that viewer's plan and answers 404 when it does not allow it. **One per member per entry** -- another replaces it, the same one removes it -- so there is nothing to accumulate, and **nothing else reads these rows**: no total, no rank, no ordering. **Aggregates only**: counts and the viewer's own choice, never a list of who reacted, which is an identity surface with its own privacy question. Both sides of a block are absent from the counts, and **nobody reacts to their own entry**, which is refused rather than hidden. Reactions on shared workouts, achievements and community activity remain for the surfaces that own them. Shipped 2026-09-20 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.54.0` and migration `20260920120000_activity_reactions`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | SOC-03, PROF-10                                        |
| SOC-06 | `SHIPPED`     | L    | Activity comments          | Discussion attached to one meaningful activity object -- a session, a record, an achievement, a challenge result -- rather than a general-purpose posting surface. Requires deletion by the author and by the activity's owner, reporting, block enforcement in both directions, the same visibility rule as the activity it hangs from, and the review queue owned by `TRUST-04`. It does not gain its own timeline, threads or profiles without a new decision. **Every dependency has shipped**; a comment is a new reportable subject kind, which `TRUST-04`'s queue takes by adding one value to `ReportSubjectKind` and one branch to its subject resolver.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | SOC-03, PROF-10, TRUST-04 (all shipped)                |
| SOC-07 | `SHIPPED`     | L    | Structured workout sharing | From a completed session's recap in history, the owner creates a public link and chooses which parts it reveals: duration, volume, completed sets and personal records by default; progression changes and session notes opt-in. Anyone with the link sees only those parts, in the owner's kg/lb unit, with the owner's name, handle and avatar and a link to the privacy-filtered public profile; the previous-session comparison is never shared. Links are unguessable, capped at ten active per session and revocable at any time, after which they stop working immediately. Shipped 2026-09-13 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.25.0` and a `SessionShare` migration; the size is corrected from `M` to `L` for that migration.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | LIVE-09, PROF-06; `@sunsteel/contracts@0.25.0`         |
| SOC-08 | `SHIPPED`     | L    | Training partners          | An explicit mutual relationship, accepted by both members and distinct from following. Each member independently grants schedule, progress, activity, shared-routine and future encouragement access; a pending request grants nothing. Schedule access returns seven dated facts without routine names or prescriptions, and the other grants only widen their matching follower-authorized reads without exposing body metrics or unrelated private profile fields. Either side can end the relationship, and blocking removes it atomically. Shipped 2026-09-22 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.57.0` and migration `20260922110000_training_partners`; encouragement delivery remains owned by `SOC-09`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | PROF-06, SOC-02, PROF-10; `@sunsteel/contracts@0.57.0` |
| SOC-09 | `SHIPPED`     | M    | Lightweight encouragement  | An active training partner may send one of four fixed prompts -- Ready to train, Strong session, Good work or Keep going -- only when the recipient's directional encouragement grant is on. Each sender-recipient pair is capped at four sends in a rolling 24 hours inside a serializable transaction, and a missing relationship, disabled grant, block or moderation hide answers 404 without disclosing which rule denied it. The existing notification row stores the prompt kind and actor, not free text or a conversation. Shipped 2026-09-23 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.58.0`; no migration.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | SOC-08, NOTIF-01, PROF-10; `@sunsteel/contracts@0.58.0` |
| SOC-10 | `CANDIDATE`   | L    | Private challenges         | A challenge between friends, training partners or specifically invited members over the `SOC-13` metrics: train on twelve distinct days this month, three sessions a week for four weeks, complete the workouts your schedule planned, improve an exercise's estimated 1RM by a defined relative amount, complete a comeback, or have every member of a small group meet a weekly target. Invitations are refusable, every participant sees the same verified evidence, and blocks are enforced before an invitation is delivered.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | SOC-13, SOC-08, PROF-10                                |
| SOC-11 | `CANDIDATE`   | L    | Public challenges          | Open, community-wide challenges over the same verified `SOC-13` metrics, joinable by any member whose privacy allows participation and leavable at any time. Standings express participation, consistency and relative personal improvement, never an absolute-strength ranking, so a challenge stays meaningful across body sizes and experience. Public participation makes reporting and the `TRUST-04` review path a prerequisite rather than an addition.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | SOC-10, SOC-13, TRUST-04                               |
| SOC-12 | `DEFERRED`    | XL   | Communities (umbrella)     | The former Training groups item, widened: a Community is a persistent social space around a shared identity or purpose -- a discipline, a gym, a coach, a creator, a locality, a club, a partner -- not a second kind of friend list. **This ID is retained as the umbrella only.** The subsystem is decomposed into `COMM-01` through `COMM-08` and is implemented there; do not open work against `SOC-12`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Decomposed; see `COMM-01`                              |
| SOC-13 | `CANDIDATE`   | L    | Challenge foundation       | One shared challenge model before any challenge surface exists: a metric, a window, a participant set, and a result derived from data the product already verifies -- completed sessions, distinct active days, adherence to the planned schedule, relative estimated-1RM improvement, comeback completion, and per-member targets inside a small group. Metrics reward participation, consistency, adherence or relative personal improvement, never absolute load. Evaluation reuses the `DATA-02`/`DATA-03` reads instead of a parallel statistics path, and a metric that cannot be derived from verifiable product data is not offered. `SOC-10` and `SOC-11` are the private and public surfaces over this one model.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | DATA-02, DATA-03, PROG-01                              |

Tables list IDs in numeric order; the delivery order inside this group is the
dependency order: `PROF-10` and `TRUST-04` first, then `SOC-03` with `SOC-04`
(activity is not generated before its visibility controls exist), then `SOC-05`
and `SOC-06` on top of that activity, then `SOC-08` and `SOC-09`.
Everything through `SOC-09` has shipped. The three challenge items below remain
candidates rather than an implied next commitment.

The three challenge items are `CANDIDATE` on purpose (decided 2026-09-16): the
direction and the metrics are settled, the timing is not, and nothing else
depends on them -- `ACH-07` is itself a candidate, `COMM-04` is deferred, and
`SOC-08` delivers the partner relationship without them. If they are approved,
the order is `SOC-13` before either surface and `SOC-10` before `SOC-11`;
`SOC-13` is numbered last because it was identified last, not because it would
ship last.

### Communities and rooms

`SOC-12` is the umbrella; this is the decomposition. A Community is a persistent
social space around a shared identity or purpose: a training discipline, a gym,
a coach, a creator, a local training community, a club, or a brand or partner.
It is a major subsystem, and every item below is written so it can be delivered
on its own once the ones before it exist.

Nothing here starts before the social core it reads: identity and privacy
(`PROF-06`), blocking and reporting (`PROF-10`), moderation (`TRUST-04`),
verified activity (`SOC-03`, `SOC-04`) and, for anything live, the realtime
transport (`MSG-06`). `COMM-04` additionally needs the challenge model
(`SOC-13`), which is a candidate rather than queued work.

| ID      | Status      | Size | Feature                          | User-facing behavior                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Dependencies                      |
| ------- | ----------- | ---- | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| COMM-01 | `DEFERRED`  | XL   | Community model and membership   | The identity and membership foundation every other `COMM-*` item reads: name, description, image, public or private membership with explicit joining rules, members, roles and moderators. It is the only item in this family that may be started first, and it defines what a community owns before anything is published into one.                                                                                                                                                                                              | PROF-06, PROF-10, TRUST-04        |
| COMM-02 | `DEFERRED`  | L    | Community activity feed          | The `SOC-03` activity of members, scoped to one community and governed by both the member's own visibility rules and the community's membership rules. A community never widens what a member chose to share.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | COMM-01, SOC-03, SOC-04           |
| COMM-03 | `DEFERRED`  | XL   | Community rooms                  | Persistent topic-based rooms inside a community -- for example `#general`, `#training`, `#nutrition`, `#books`, `#events` -- carrying native Sunnsteel objects as well as text: a verified Bench Press record shared into `#training` keeps a link back to the workout it came from, still subject to the owner's privacy. Requires per-room permissions, moderation, reporting, block enforcement and the `MSG-06` transport. Supersedes `MSG-04`.                                                                               | COMM-01, MSG-06, TRUST-04         |
| COMM-04 | `DEFERRED`  | L    | Community challenges             | Challenges owned by a community over the verified `SOC-13` metrics, open to its members under its membership rules.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | COMM-01, SOC-13                   |
| COMM-05 | `DEFERRED`  | L    | Community routines and content   | Routines and curated content published by a community through the existing `ROUT-04` sharing model and the `LIB-01` ownership model, never a second parallel content system.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | COMM-01, ROUT-04, LIB-01          |
| COMM-06 | `DEFERRED`  | M    | Announcements and events         | Community announcements and dated events, delivered through the existing notification categories and controls rather than a channel of their own.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | COMM-01, NOTIF-05                 |
| COMM-07 | `DEFERRED`  | L    | Official and partner communities | Selected organizations, coaches, creators or partners operate an official space inside Sunnsteel: verified identity, an official community profile, community challenges, curated routines, collections, announcements, events and moderation roles. Verification and enforcement are product decisions before they are code. **No partner may become a dependency of the core product:** any official community can be removed without touching anything outside this family, and none of them defines Sunnsteel's own identity. | COMM-01 through COMM-06, TRUST-04 |
| COMM-08 | `CANDIDATE` | L    | Standalone public rooms          | Public thematic rooms not owned by any community -- Strength training, Bodybuilding, Running, Nutrition, Philosophy, Books -- with no persistent organization, membership hierarchy, challenges or programs of their own. **Deliberately held as a candidate:** until `COMM-01` defines membership, roles and moderation, a second discussion primitive would overlap community rooms and split moderation in two. Re-evaluate after `COMM-03` ships, not before.                                                                 | COMM-01, COMM-03                  |

Decide the overlap before writing code, not after: `COMM-03` and `COMM-08` are
the same primitive with different ownership, and the product should end up with
one discussion model, one moderation path and one object-sharing format, not
two.

Achievements shows the current rank, attendance evidence and exact remaining requirements for the next rank. | ACH-05 | `SHIPPED`   | M    | Comeback recognition     | Achievements derives comeback evidence from the 500 most recent immutable completed-session events. After 14 full inactive local-calendar days, three distinct active dates inside an inclusive 14-day return window record a comeback; same-day sessions count once. The page explains the rule, links each recognition to its third return session, and discloses truncated history. Shipped 2026-09-15 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.42.0`; no migration or backfill.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | DATA-02                                  |

Shipped `PROG-08` already stores and evaluates up to eight private measurable targets, so this is a generated-suggestion mode inside that model -- the product proposes, the owner accepts, and it becomes an ordinary goal -- never a second target system with its own storage and evaluator.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | PROG-08, SCHED-01                        |
| ACH-07 | `CANDIDATE` | M    | Seasonal campaigns       | Run optional themed challenges without resetting permanent progress.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | SOC-11, SOC-13                           |
| ACH-08 | `SHIPPED`   | M    | Achievements on profiles | Every member profile has a complete Achievements section with the current rank, earned milestones and comeback recognitions under the existing private-by-default `achievements` visibility rule. The backend resolves access before the achievement read; authenticated profiles apply owner/follower/public access and signed-out profiles receive the ledger only when `PUBLIC`. Earned items retain their date/session or explicit historical provenance, while next-milestone progress and rank requirements remain owner-only on `/achievements`. | ACH-09 | `SHIPPED`   | S    | Rank identity            | Shipped 2026-09-23. Each of the six ranks has a crest and a pigment keyed by the contract's stable rank ID, on `/achievements`, the `ACH-08` profile section, the `ACH-03` featured rank row, the Settings rank picker, the `DASH-09` next-rank milestone and the generated `PROF-11` profile card. Design system §19 adds six mark-grade tokens (silverpoint, verdigris, azurite, ultramarine, folium, Tyrian purple) for both themes, clear of `honour`, `success`, `warning` and `destructive`, and one *testa di cavallo* crest family that gains an ornament at every rank. The crest never appears without the rank name, a rank not yet reached is drawn in `ink-3`, and the `/achievements` rank title no longer borrows `honour`. | ACH-02; design-system amendment          |

### Notifications and retention

| ID       | Status      | Size | Feature                    | User-facing behavior                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Dependencies                           |
| -------- | ----------- | ---- | -------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| NOTIF-01 | `SHIPPED`   | L    | In-app notification center | A bell in the top bar leads to a Notifications page; unread notifications change its glyph and accessible name. Today lists the live session to resume or today's planned workouts to start, without read state. Updates lists, newest first, achievements earned live (never ones recognized from history), one note per finished session that set records or changed loads, and new followers from the last 30 days, gathered on read from records that already exist. Opening one marks it read, and all can be marked read at once. Shipped 2026-09-15 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.43.0` and migration `20260915230000_notifications`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | DATA-02, PROF-06                       |
| NOTIF-02 | `SHIPPED`   | M    | Web Push foundation        | Settings gains a Notifications card that states its own availability and raises the permission prompt only from a click: a prompt on load spends the one chance an origin gets. Every unavailable case names its reason instead of hiding -- an unsupported browser, a server with no keys, a browser that already refused, and, checked before all of them, an iOS device that has not installed Sunnsteel to the Home Screen, where Web Push does nothing in a tab. The service worker gained `push`, `notificationclick` (focusing an open session rather than opening a second copy) and `pushsubscriptionchange` handlers. Shipped 2026-09-17, frontend only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | NOTIF-08; service-worker push handlers |
| NOTIF-03 | `SHIPPED`   | S    | Rest-timer alert           | The end of a rest period now reaches a locked phone. **The alert is a notification, not the in-app tone** -- `LIVE-01` synthesises that tone with WebAudio inside the page, and a backgrounded or locked PWA has its audio context suspended, so only the OS can ring. The body is a static line naming the lift that is up next; the scope limit holds, because the Web Notifications API has no chronometer field and nothing promises a countdown. One pending alert per session rather than one per completed set: a new rest period replaces the last, extending the timer reschedules it and dismissing it cancels. A pending alert is a persisted row swept by an existing `@nestjs/schedule` interval and claimed with `DELETE ... RETURNING`, so a restart cannot lose it and two instances cannot double-send. Refusals are stated (`NO_SUBSCRIPTION`, `PUSH_UNAVAILABLE`, `TOO_SOON`) rather than reported as success, and scheduling is only attempted for accounts with a subscribed device. Shipped 2026-09-17.                                                                                                      | LIVE-01, NOTIF-02                      |
| NOTIF-04 | `SHIPPED`   | M    | Training reminders         | A reminder on the days an account is planned to train, **at a time the owner picks rather than before a session**: a routine day carries a weekday and nothing stores an intended hour, so counting back from a session would mean inventing its time, and deriving one from history would present a guess as a schedule. The planned-day rule is the Schedule page's, ported to the server because nobody has the app open when it fires -- archived routines and rotations without training weekdays plan nothing, a rest day is a plan not to train, moves and skips apply, and a date already trained is dropped. The planner ticks every five minutes, matches the current local minute against a ten-minute catch-up window so a restart does not lose the day, and enqueues one push per account per local date. Shipped 2026-09-17 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.47.0` and migration `20260917140000_notification_preferences`.                                                                                                                                                                                           | SCHED-01, PREF-04, NOTIF-02            |
| NOTIF-05 | `SHIPPED`   | M    | Notification controls      | Rest alerts and training reminders switch independently, and a quiet window silences both, including one that runs overnight. Both are applied in a single place at **send** rather than at schedule, so an alert set before quiet hours begin still stops, and a category switched off afterwards also deletes what was already waiting. Settings states the one thing worth knowing above the controls -- no server keys, no subscribed device, or every category off -- because a switch left on with nothing to deliver it reads as working. **Channels and digests were deliberately not built:** Web Push is the only delivery channel, so a channel switch would be the category switch under another name, and the NOTIF-01 centre is gathered on read rather than delivered; a digest of two notification types would batch nothing worth batching, and the row called it optional. Shipped 2026-09-17.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | NOTIF-01, NOTIF-02                     |
| NOTIF-06 | `SHIPPED`   | S    | Streak-at-risk reminder    | A nudge on the last local date a training run can still be continued, derived rather than invented: `nextStreak` continues a run while the gap between training dates is at most `STREAK_MAX_GAP_DAYS` (3), so the run ends after that and the last date that can extend it is exactly three days past the last session. It fires on that day only -- earlier, nothing is at stake; later, the run has already gone and announcing it would be untrue -- and never when a session has been logged that day. It is a third switchable category riding the `NOTIF-04` reminder time rather than a delivery path of its own, and it **replaces** that day's training reminder instead of sending a second push. It is evaluated whether or not the day is planned, which is what makes it worth having beside the reminder. **It states the dates and never instructs anyone to train**, because a rest day ending a streak is the plan working rather than a failure; a test asserts the copy carries no imperative. Shipped 2026-09-17 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.48.0` and migration `20260917170000_streak_at_risk_category`. | NOTIF-04, NOTIF-05, DATA-02            |
| NOTIF-07 | `CANDIDATE` | M    | Partner activity alerts    | Notify only for selected partner events and respect both users' privacy preferences.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | SOC-08, NOTIF-05                       |
| NOTIF-08 | `SHIPPED`   | L    | Push delivery service      | Per-device push subscriptions, VAPID credentials held by the server, payloads signed and sent to every device of an account, and a subscription retired only on a definitive 404 or 410 -- a 429 or 5xx may succeed next time. Registration is idempotent on the endpoint, the newest `PUSH_SUBSCRIPTIONS_MAX` devices are kept, and **the endpoint is never returned to a client** because it is the capability to push to that device. A deployment without VAPID keys runs unchanged and reports push unavailable, so no client is prompted for a permission that could not be honoured. Shipped 2026-09-17 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.46.0`, migration `20260917074329_push_delivery` and the `web-push` dependency.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | BE/CT subscription model               |

### Preferences, accessibility, and trust

| ID        | Status      | Size | Feature                               | User-facing behavior                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Dependencies                          |
| --------- | ----------- | ---- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------- |

A moderator account is refused, because the append-only moderation record cascades from its moderator. Copies other members made of a shared routine stay theirs with the lineage cleared, and reports **about** the member stay for the queue. The confirmation states what goes, what stays and that no export exists yet (`EXPORT-01`); afterwards Login says the account was deleted. | TRUST-04  | `SHIPPED`   | L    | Moderation foundation                 | The report queue, review actions, enforcement records and audit trail every member-visible interaction reads instead of building its own. A single moderator flag on the account is the whole authorization model; a review may dismiss a report or hide the reported member, routine or shared workout from everyone but its owner, and **nothing is deleted**. The queue resolves each subject through the shipped `PROF-06`/`PROF-10` rules **as the reviewer**, so a subject they could not otherwise open is reported as withheld rather than revealed, and reading one is itself recorded. Enforcement records are append-only: undoing a hide appends a restore rather than editing the hide. Shipped 2026-09-21 through `@sunsteel/contracts@0.55.0` and migration `20260920160000_moderation_foundation`. | PROF-10; `@sunsteel/contracts@0.55.0` |

### Content library and development

The DEVELOP layer of the product evolution: long-term candidates, not approved
delivery. Training remains the measurable foundation, and nothing here replaces
it. The order matters more than the ambition -- `LIB-01` is the model every
other content surface in the roadmap has been missing an owner for, including
the exercise instructions and media in `EXER-03` and `EXER-04`.

| ID     | Status      | Size | Feature                     | User-facing behavior                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Dependencies                             |
| ------ | ----------- | ---- | --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------- |
| LIB-01 | `CANDIDATE` | XL   | Content model and authoring | A content item with an owner, an author, a publication state, a review path and a removal path. Every content feature in the roadmap needs it -- `LIB-02`, `LIB-03`, `COMM-05`, and the exercise instructions and media of `EXER-03`/`EXER-04` -- and without it each one invents its own ownership, licensing and moderation. This is the ID for the previously unowned "content-authoring process" dependency.                                                                                       | TRUST-04; licensing and authoring policy |
| LIB-02 | `CANDIDATE` | XL   | Content library             | A structured library of useful long-form or curated material across Training (programming, technique, recovery, nutrition), Mind (focus, discipline, resilience), Culture (books, essays, art, history) and Practices (sleep, walking, reading, training, reflection). It is a finite library with a stated source, **not an infinite short-form feed**, and nothing in it is presented as medical advice. Content may come from Sunnsteel, verified contributors or official communities (`COMM-07`). | LIB-01                                   |
| LIB-03 | `CANDIDATE` | L    | Collections                 | Finite, ordered paths through library content -- for example `The Physical Foundation`: strength, programming, recovery, food, sleep, reading, community. A collection has an end and shows where the reader is in it, without streaks, deadlines or engagement pressure. Collections may be authored by Sunnsteel or by approved official communities.                                                                                                                                                | LIB-02                                   |

## Product rules and retained decisions

- Privacy is designed before activity feeds, public routines, comments, coaching,
  or messaging. Sensitive profile and body data is private by default.
- Progress, achievements, feeds, and notifications build on persistent records,
  events, rollups, and truthful historical snapshots—not repeated lifetime scans.
- Estimated 1RM per lift is the headline strength trend. Total volume is useful
  mainly in exercise and muscle-group context.
- Ranks, leaderboards and challenges favor consistency, participation,
  adherence and relative improvement rather than absolute load, so they remain
  meaningful across body sizes and experience. A challenge result is derived
  from verifiable product data whenever possible; a metric the product cannot
  verify is not offered.
- The active-session screen prioritizes speed, legibility, offline resilience,
  and one-handed use over decorative complexity.
- **Activity comes from doing, not posting.** Social activity is generated from
  verified training events -- sessions, records, achievements, rank progression,
  streaks, comebacks, shared routines, challenge progress -- with explicit
  per-type sharing controls. Users are never required to author conventional
  social posts, and a free-text composer is not the answer to a thin feed.
- Reaction sets stay small and themed. Reactions acknowledge work; they never
  become a score, never affect ranks, records, challenge results or ordering,
  and the catalog does not grow to imitate other platforms' gamification.
- Comments and discussion attach to a meaningful activity object, not to a
  general-purpose posting surface. Deletion, reporting, block enforcement and
  the activity's own visibility rule are part of the first version, not a later
  addition.
- **One primitive per job.** Before adding a social surface, check whether an
  existing one already covers it: community rooms and standalone public rooms,
  clubs and communities, group discussion and rooms are the cases already
  caught. The product should end up with one discussion model, one moderation
  path and one object-sharing format.
- **Partners live inside the platform; the platform does not live inside a
  partner.** Official communities may be operated by outside organizations,
  coaches or creators, but no external organization may become a dependency of
  the core product, and removing one must not reach outside the `COMM-*` family.
- Messaging is not an early social feature. It remains deferred until identity,
  privacy, blocking, moderation, notifications, and delivery infrastructure
  exist. When it does arrive it carries Sunnsteel objects -- workouts, routines,
  exercises, records, achievements, challenges, progress summaries -- so it
  extends the training platform instead of adding a generic chat beside it.
- Exercise instructions and demonstrations require a maintained content and
  licensing process; engineering alone is not sufficient. `LIB-01` owns that
  model for the whole product, so `EXER-03`, `EXER-04`, `LIB-02` and `COMM-05`
  share one ownership, review and removal path instead of inventing three.
- Curated content is finite and sourced. A library and its collections give
  intentional paths with an end, not an infinite short-form feed, and nothing in
  them is presented as medical advice.
- Automated training recommendations explain their evidence and require user
  confirmation. They do not diagnose injuries, recovery state, or health issues.
  **Show evidence first. Do not invent causes.** Stating that an exercise has
  produced no new estimated-1RM best across a stated number of sessions, and
  then offering reading on programming, recovery, sleep or nutrition, is
  allowed; concluding a medical condition, hormonal status, CNS fatigue or a
  specific recovery problem is not.
- Lifestyle expansion is additive and sequenced: TRAIN, then TRAIN + CONNECT,
  then TRAIN + CONNECT + DEVELOP. Training remains the measurable foundation of
  the product at every stage, and a later layer never replaces it or degrades
  it.
- Large social and content systems are decomposed into independently
  deliverable features with their own IDs before implementation starts, and the
  foundations they need -- identity, privacy, blocking, reporting, moderation,
  notifications and reliable activity data -- ship first. Do not start a social
  or lifestyle direction as one monolithic initiative.
- Catalog stars (`EXER-07`) are private working preferences in their own
  model. The `PROF-05` profile favorites are identity metadata governed by
  profile privacy and must not be repurposed as a browsing or wizard
  preference, and stars must never appear on a profile.
- An interim fix that replaces one false promise with another is not an
  improvement. When the honest short version of a feature would still mislead,
  leave the surface visibly unfinished and wait for the real capability. This
  rule comes from the `FIX-05` decision on 2026-09-06 and applies to any
  half-shipped social or sharing surface.

## Rejected features

No product feature has been formally rejected yet. When one is rejected, retain
its stable ID here with the date and reason so a future session does not propose
it again without addressing the original decision.

The consequence was verified rather than assumed: **after hiding a routine the moderator who hid it can no longer read it either**, which is correct -- the enforcement record, not a privileged read, is how a decision is revisited. **Reading is an action.** Opening a subject writes a `VIEW_SUBJECT` record before the answer and the reviewer then opens the subject's own page; there is deliberately no mirror of the content, because a second read path is a second answer to "may this viewer see this" and the one that drifts is the one nobody is looking at. **Hiding narrows the shipped rule instead of adding a gate beside it.** `canViewRoutine` gained a required `moderation` argument, which is what made the compiler name every read that had to consider it; a hidden routine is refused before either `ROUT-04` rule is asked, a hidden share stops resolving for its token while `revokedAt` stays null, and a hidden account answers 404 and leaves search, suggestions, relationship lists, activity and discovery. **Nothing is deleted**: the routine keeps its days, versions and links, the share keeps its token, the account keeps everything it owns and still reads its own profile. **Blocks and hides are answered together** by one pair of helpers replacing five duplicated block lookups, so a read written later cannot honour one and omit the other. **Undoing a hide appends rather than edits.** A `RESTORE_SUBJECT` record supersedes the `HIDE_SUBJECT` one, which stays exactly as written; Hiding the member answered 404 on the unguarded profile, the authenticated profile, the routine list and the activity read and removed them from search; hiding the share stopped the token resolving while `revokedAt` stayed null. With the flag revoked the queue and the record both answered **404**, and unauthenticated they answered 401. **Browser, one context:** the page rendered its scope line, its no-bypass note and the three states; the hide dialog stated that the owner keeps the content, that nothing is deleted and that it can be restored; confirming dropped the open count and toasted that the action was recorded and cannot be edited; the Content hidden tab then showed "Routine · not visible to you" with no title, no owner and only Restore; and the Record tab listed every action including the recorded read. **One copy defect was found in the browser and fixed in the same slice:** the withheld note ended "You can still dismiss the report or hide the content", which on an already-hidden row named two controls it was not showing. | 2026-09-22 | SOC-06 | CT `@sunsteel/contracts@0.56.0` (`ActivityCommentSummary` inside every entry, `ActivityComment`, the list/create/delete shapes, `ReportSubjectKind.COMMENT`, `NotificationKind.ACTIVITY_COMMENT`, published to npm); BE `ActivityComment` and migration `20260921140000_activity_comments` registered in `scripts/prepare-analytics-test-db.ts`, the comment rules in `activity-rules.ts`, `listComments`/`createComment`/`deleteComment` behind `resolveCommentableEntry`, the `COMMENT` branch in `moderation.service.ts`, the comment source in `notification-sources.ts` (+ 16 tests, 321 in total); FE `features/activity/activity-comments.tsx`, the comment hooks and cache patch, the notification copy and icon, and the `COMMENT` moderation labels (+ 4 tests, 497 across seventy-one files). Anyone who may read the entry may comment on it -- a second permission would be a second answer to the same question -- and unlike a reaction the owner is allowed through, because replying on your own activity is ordinary where acknowledging your own work is not. **Two people may delete**: the author, and the owner of the activity it hangs from; a moderator is deliberately not one of them, because `TRUST-04` hides rather than deletes and the enforcement record stays the only account of what a moderator did. **The count never disagrees with the list**: both sides of a block and every hidden comment are excluded from each, so a number can never advertise a comment the viewer cannot open. **The notification carries no body.** A notification row outlives what it describes -- its author, the recipient and a moderator can each remove a comment -- so a quoted copy could be withdrawn by none of them; the row names the commenter and the entry. The owner saw `canDelete: true` on the seeded member's comment on their own activity, deleted it, and got 404 deleting it again and 404 on an id that was never theirs.
