| 2026-09-23 | ROUT-15 | CT [`02d8d03`](https://github.com/ezee969/sunnsteel-contracts/commit/02d8d0314ada5cf7f4b7999b7290e561564f38a5) published `@sunsteel/contracts@0.64.0` (`resolveRoutinePlan`, `RoutineTrainingBlockPlan`, `SessionTrainingBlock`, `Routine.trainingBlocks`, `WorkoutSession.trainingBlock`), CI 35915184952; BE [`46ae015`](https://github.com/ezee969/sunnsteel-backend/commit/46ae0159e4151bc2dc4a5687781a481e0514f9e8): block working copies, migration `20260923210000_training_block_execution` (backfill of existing revisions, partial weekday indexes, session block columns), plan-checked start, block-aware reminders, partner schedule and overrides, per-plan rotation and the mid-session revision guard (+8 tests, 411 total in 49 files), CI 35916376591 green including migrations and analytics integration, Railway deployment `81bca46d` SUCCESS with the migration applied in the pre-deploy hook; FE [`c1027f0`](https://github.com/ezee969/sunnsteel-frontend/commit/c1027f065dfc231c919b945e120b129edb26ea26): `routineOn`, segmented schedule, block-aware dashboard, routine card and page, list start and Train another day, snapshot-backed session screen and block names in history (+13 tests, 579 total in 84 files), CI 35917792562 green, GitHub Production deployment `success` and the `sunnsteel-frontend.vercel.app` `/schedule` chunk serving the new block copy | **Design decided with the owner:** progression advances inside the block (its working copy), not the baseline; only the block's days start while it is in force, and the server enforces it; reminders and the partner schedule follow the block. A setup has no ids while every session path is keyed by real routine-exercise ids, so blocks execute through materialized working copies rather than synthetic slots, which kept set logging, finish progression, previous performance, LIVE-11, LIVE-16 and LIVE-17 unchanged at the cost of a `trainingBlockId: null` filter on every baseline read. **Real-stack verified 2026-09-23 on a copy of the local database** (Codex was testing PROF-11 against the shared one): the migration applied twice and its backfill re-ran without duplicating (the two seeded blocks each gained 4 days, 40 exercises); with "Autumn accumulation" in force, a baseline day was refused 409 with the block named, a block day started and the session carried the block, revising the block mid-session was refused 409, and finishing moved the block's working-copy load 77.5 → 80 kg while the baseline stayed 77.5. **Browser (same copy, :3100/:4100, a borrowed never-refreshed session):** the routine card and page named the block ("in force until Oct 12, 2026 … return on Oct 13, 2026"), the schedule named it beside its days and switched to "Strength intensification" three weeks on, history named it, and a block day started from the routine page opened a session titled with the block and training its snapshot day, with no console errors. The dashboard block line was not seen because that routine had already been trained that day. **Scoped sweep:** 96/98 on `schedule`, `dashboard`, `routines`, `routine-detail`, `workouts`, `history` and `session`; the two failures were `session @ 320`, a 7 px overflow from an exercise row whose title could not shrink beside three controls, fixed in the same commit, after which `session` passed 14/14. The full 480 was not run. **Not verified:** a production-origin authenticated pass; a rotation block and a MOVE across a block boundary in a browser (both unit-tested); reminders firing during a block (unit-tested); the brief window between the backend and frontend deploys, when the old frontend could offer a baseline day during a block and receive the 409. Blocks are still not in the account export. |

## Document history

- **2026-09-06:** Created the canonical product registry from a review of the
  frontend, backend, shared contracts, existing technical-debt roadmap, and the
  combined feature brainstorm.
- **2026-09-06 (revision 2):** Corrected `DATA-01`-`DATA-05` to `IN_PROGRESS`
  against the verified analytics implementation and recorded its rollout gates;
  reordered the active queue so it describes real state; split the integrity
  section by size and reassigned the feature-sized parts of `FIX-04` and
  `FIX-06` to `LIVE-06` and `SOC-02`; clarified that `FIX-03` is a presentation
  concern because canonical storage is already kilograms; and gave the two
  recurring hidden blockers their own IDs (`EXER-09`, `NOTIF-08`). Other
  unowned dependency-column items -- schedule-instance model, moderation
  tooling, content authoring, accessibility audit, data inventory -- still lack
  IDs.
- **2026-09-06 (revision 3):** Added a structural `Size` column to every feature
  table and to the active queue, with a legend defining size by reach rather
  than duration. Translated [technical-debt.md](technical-debt.md) to English so
  both active registers match the language of the code and the four
  `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` files, and repointed the `TD-27` link at a stable
  anchor. The July 2026 audit in `docs/history/` stays in Spanish as frozen
  evidence.
- **2026-09-06 (revision 4):** Deferred `FIX-05` instead of shipping an interim
  share fix, after confirming that `/profile` sits behind the middleware and a
  shared link would strand signed-out visitors on `/login`. Added `PROF-12`
  (public profile route and sharing) to own the real capability, added it to
  queue group 5 and to `PROF-11`'s dependencies, and recorded the general rule
  in the retained decisions. The honesty group is now six frontend-only fixes:
  `FIX-01`, `FIX-02`, `FIX-04`, `FIX-06`, `FIX-07`, `FIX-08`.
- **2026-09-07:** Recorded the Quick Workout decision: `FIX-04` hides the entry
  point rather than seeding it, and `LIVE-06` moves to `BLOCKED` because its
  replacement flow is not designed yet. Noted the template-selection direction
  and its overlap with `ROUT-03`, whose `Curated templates` dependency still has
  no owning ID.
- **2026-09-08:** Added `LIVE-19` (discard an in-progress session), found while
  restoring account state during the UI-restyle baseline capture: a session
  started for a screenshot could not be abandoned through the UI, only finished.
  Filed as `S` because every layer below the missing control already exists and
  is already exercised by the stale-session dialog.
- **2026-09-08 (revision 2):** Added `FIX-09` and `FIX-10`, both spotted in the
  UI-restyle baseline screenshots and both out of scope for a restyle because
  they are formatting logic rather than styling. `FIX-09` is the more useful
  find: it is not a missing feature but two duration formatters coexisting and
  disagreeing, which is why the same magnitude renders differently on two
  screens.
  Queue group 2 had been marked complete in the meantime; it reopens with these
  two, which were never part of the shipped set.
- **2026-09-07 (revision 2):** Shipped the six frontend-only honesty fixes as a
  single change -- `FIX-01`, `FIX-02`, `FIX-04`, `FIX-06`, `FIX-07`, `FIX-08` --
  and moved them to `SHIPPED`. Queue group 2 now holds only `FIX-03`. Three
  decisions are worth carrying forward. `FIX-01` removed the invented biography
  and location but kept the About block with a one-line honest empty state,
  because the capability is wanted and `PROF-04` owns the real editable fields;
  it did not build an empty state elaborate enough for `PROF-04` to discard.
  `FIX-02` deleted the badge outright rather than conditioning it, because
  neither `UserProfile` nor `PublicUserProfile` carries a membership, plan or
  tier field to condition it on. `FIX-04` hid the entry point behind a
  `SHOW_QUICK_WORKOUT_ENTRY` flag instead of deleting the flow, so `LIVE-06`
  resumes from working code once its product decision is made.
- **2026-09-07 (revision 5):** Recorded the scope limit on `NOTIF-03` after the
  capability was asked for directly: the end-of-rest **alert** is reachable
  through `NOTIF-08` and `NOTIF-02`, but a **visible countdown on a locked**
  **screen** is not a web capability and no amount of work in this stack
  delivers it. The row previously said only "notify when rest ends", which was
  correct but left the impossible half unaddressed and available to be promised

- **2026-09-07 (revision 4):** Closed the analytics foundation against verified

- **2026-09-14 (revision 57):** Shipped `PROG-09` and released its claim.

- **2026-09-14 (revision 58):** Added and claimed `PREF-05` for Claude at the

- **2026-09-14 (revision 59):** Shipped `PREF-05` and released its claim.
  The plateau watch's minimum sessions is now an account preference (3–8,

- **2026-09-14 (revision 60):** Claimed `ROUT-11` for Claude with the
  owner's approved design: each routine is either weekly (days tied to
  weekdays, as today) or a rotation (ordered days with no weekday); every day
  may carry its own name in either mode; a rotation's next day follows the
  last completed session of that routine, an aborted session does not
  advance it, and the dashboard shows the next rotation day instead of a
  weekday match. It changes the routine model, hence `L`, and unblocks
  `SCHED-01` and `SCHED-06`.
- **2026-09-14 (revision 61):** Shipped `ROUT-11` and released its claim.
  Routines are weekly or a rotation, days may be named, and a rotation offers
  its next day on any weekday from the backend's `nextRotationDayId`. This
  unblocks `SCHED-01` (weekly calendar) and `SCHED-06` (recurring and rotating
  plans); `SCHED-01` is the next dependency-ready schedule slice.
- **2026-09-14 (revision 62):** Claimed `SCHED-01` for Claude. The owner
  delegated its three open rules, decided as follows. A past weekly day with
  no session reads "Not logged" in neutral styling, never "missed", and only
  from the routine's creation date on; rotation days have no date, so they
  are never "not logged". Rest days are neither inferred nor stored: a day
  with nothing planned says so, and intentional recovery stays with
  `SCHED-07`. Rotations are not projected onto future dates; the week shows
  each rotation's next day as a note, and its sessions on the days they
  happened.
- **2026-09-14 (revision 63):** Shipped `SCHED-01` and released its claim.
  The Schedule page is live in the sidebar with the rules from revision 62.
  `SCHED-03` (start from calendar) and `SCHED-07` (rest-day planning) are
  now dependency-ready; `SCHED-02` also waits on the monthly consistency view
  it names.
- **2026-09-14 (revision 64):** Claimed `SCHED-03` for Claude. The
  Schedule page will start today's planned weekly days and each rotation's
  next day, and resume the live session, under the same start rules the
  routine card and dashboard already apply; frontend only.
- **2026-09-14 (revision 65):** Shipped `SCHED-03` and released its claim.
  The Schedule page starts today's work and resumes the live session.
  `SCHED-07` (rest-day planning) is the next dependency-ready schedule item
  and needs its own product rules (how rest is marked and whether it counts
  in the week) before it starts.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 66):** Claimed `SCHED-07` for Claude and resized
  it to `L`, since it stores a new routine field. The owner delegated its
  rules, decided as follows. Rest is marked by weekday on each weekly
  routine, as part of that program; per-date rest would need the
  schedule-instance model of `SCHED-04`. A planned rest day reads "Rest day"
  on the schedule before and after today and is never "not logged"; a
  session of that routine on the day replaces it. Rotations get no rest
  days: they advance only on completed sessions, so a rest day inside the
  cycle would need an invented "rest done" state, and rest in a rotation is
  simply the days you do not train.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 67):** Shipped `SCHED-07` and released its claim.
  Weekly routines carry planned rest days, and the schedule shows them. The
  remaining schedule items need the schedule-instance model (`SCHED-04`,
  `SCHED-05`) or a monthly view with its own consistency rules (`SCHED-02`).
- **2026-09-15 (revision 68):** Claimed `PROG-05` and `SCHED-02` together
  for Claude: one monthly consistency calendar, shown on the Schedule page
  and in Progress, built from the same week rules. It needs no new rule:
  "missed" in `PROG-05` reads "Not logged" as decided for `SCHED-01`, and its

- **2026-09-15 (revision 69):** Shipped `ACH-04` and released its claim.
  Published `@sunsteel/contracts@0.32.0` and extended the existing achievement
  read with one next fixed milestone per category, derived from the same
  verified totals as the earned ledger. The presentation keeps catalog order,
  uses exact evidence without percentages or deadlines, and preserves planned
  loads and recovery as the safety boundary. The local sweep also exposed and

  idempotent inserts. Production passed the complete responsive/theme matrix,
  and all 395 local regression cases passed across the documented recovery
  runs. No migration or one-off backfill was needed. `ACH-05` is the next

- **2026-09-15 (revision 70):** Shipped `PROG-05` and `SCHED-02` and
  released their claims. One monthly calendar serves the Schedule page and
  Progress, completing the Progress destination group. The remaining
  schedule items need the schedule-instance model (`SCHED-04`, `SCHED-05`)
  or belong to push notifications.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 71):** Claimed `ROUT-08` for Claude. The owner
  delegated its rules, decided as follows. A version is an immutable copy of
  the whole routine setup (schedule mode, rest days, days, exercises, sets,
  loads, progression, rest and notes), saved intentionally with an optional
  name; restoring first saves the current setup as a "Before restore"
  version, so a restore can always be undone. A version is compared with the
  current routine day by day, exercises matched by exercise within a day,
  because every edit replaces the stored days. Restoring replaces the setup,
  loads included (undoing later automatic progression, which the
  confirmation shows), keeps the routine, its favorite flag and its history,
  and is refused while a session of the routine is in progress. A routine
  keeps at most 20 versions, and any can be deleted. Contracts, backend
  (a new table) and frontend.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 72):** Shipped `ROUT-08` and released its claim.
  Routines keep intentional versions that can be compared and restored.
  `ROUT-09` (training blocks and deloads) is now dependency-ready and needs
  its own product rules before it starts.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 73):** Claimed `SCHED-06` for Claude. `ROUT-11`
  already delivered weekday schedules and non-weekly rotations, so the owner
  chose to scope the rest as rotations on fixed training weekdays; the owner
  delegated the details, decided as follows. A rotation may choose the
  weekdays it trains on (for example Monday, Wednesday and Friday); without
  any it behaves as before, on any day and without dates. With weekdays, the
  Schedule projects the rotation from today: its next day falls on the first
  training weekday from today without a session of the routine, and the
  following days on the training weekdays after it. A past training weekday
  without a session of the routine reads "Not logged", with no day name,
  because which day it would have been is not known. Any rotation day can
  still be started on any day, and the rotation still advances only on
  completed sessions. Weekly routines are unchanged, rotations keep no rest
  days (the other weekdays are simply not training days), the routine card
  and dashboard give the next day its date, and versions (`ROUT-08`) keep
  the weekdays. Contracts, backend and frontend.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 74):** Shipped `SCHED-06` and released its claim.
  Rotations can train on chosen weekdays and appear on the schedule. The
  remaining schedule items need the schedule-instance model (`SCHED-04`,
  `SCHED-05`) or belong to push notifications.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 75):** Claimed `SCHED-04` for Claude. The owner
  delegated its rules, decided as follows. It introduces the
  schedule-instance model as per-date overrides on a routine, so `SCHED-05`
  (skip and postpone) can add its own kind later. An occurrence is a weekly
  routine's planned day on one date; it can be moved to another date up to
  six days before or after, neither date in the past, and not onto a date
  where that routine is already planned. Moving again changes the target;
  undoing restores the plan. Rotations are not moved: since `SCHED-06` their
  plan re-flows from the next day on its own. On the Schedule the original
  date reads "Moved to <day>" (never "not logged") and the target date
  carries the planned day, marked as moved, with Start when it is today; a
  session of the routine on either date completes the occurrence, and a
  moved day on a planned rest day replaces the rest. The dashboard follows
  the moves for today. Overrides are stored by calendar date, not by routine
  day, because routine edits replace the days. Contracts, backend (a new
  table) and frontend.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 76):** Shipped `SCHED-04` and released its claim.
  One planned workout can move to another day through the new per-date
  override model. `SCHED-05` (skip and postpone) is now dependency-ready and
  adds its own override kinds; it needs its rules decided before it starts.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 77):** Claimed `SCHED-05` for Claude. The owner
  delegated its rules, decided as follows. Skipping adds a `SKIP` kind to
  the `SCHED-04` overrides: a weekly routine's planned day marked skipped
  reads "Skipped" in neutral styling, never "not logged", and can be undone.
  It can be marked ahead of time or, to explain a day that passed, for an
  occurrence up to six days back; a session of the routine on that date
  still counts. Skipping a moved workout skips the occurrence, and a skipped
  day is free for another workout of the routine to move onto. Postponing
  is a one-tap move to the first free day after where the workout sits,
  under the `SCHED-04` limits, so it needs no kind of its own. Rotations
  are neither skipped nor postponed: their plan re-flows on its own. The
  dashboard drops a workout skipped for today. Contracts, backend (a new
  enum value) and frontend.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 78):** Shipped `SCHED-05` and released its claim.
  A planned weekly workout can be skipped, ahead of time or for a day that
  passed, and postponed in one tap; a skip never reads as not logged. Every
  `SCHED-*` item has now shipped; the rest of group 9 is push notifications.
- **2026-09-15 (revision 79):** Claimed `NOTIF-01` for Claude. The owner
  delegated its rules, decided as follows. The center is a Notifications page
  reached from a bell in the top bar, whose glyph and accessible name show
  unread notifications; restrained sidebar and schedule indicators stay with
  `NAV-05`. Stored notifications carry read state and are gathered, when the
  list is read, from records that already exist rather than hooked into each
  write path: an achievement earned live (never one recognized from history),
  one note per finished session that set records or changed loads, and a new
  follower. Each source reaches back at most 30 days. Opening a notification
  marks it read, and all can be marked read at once. Above them, a Today
  section lists what can be acted on now, without read state: today's planned
  workouts with Start and a live session with Resume. Push delivery,
  reminders and per-category controls stay with `NOTIF-02` to `NOTIF-05`.
  Contracts, backend (a new table) and frontend.

- **2026-09-17 (revision 97):** Claimed `NOTIF-08`, `NOTIF-02` and `NOTIF-03` for
  Claude as one slice across contracts, backend and frontend. Codex has stopped
  working on this product, so the slice is no longer shaped around avoiding
  another agent's modules. The three are claimed together because `NOTIF-03` is
  the only one a user can feel and it is `S` solely on the assumption that
  `NOTIF-08` already exists; shipping the delivery service without the alert
  would leave the whole group unverifiable in the one situation it exists for.
  The scope limits `NOTIF-03` already records stand: a static line such as
  `Rest ends at 14:32`, never a ticking countdown, and on iOS only in a PWA
  installed to the Home Screen. Scheduling uses a persisted due-row swept by an
  existing `@nestjs/schedule` interval rather than a new queue, so a restart
  cannot lose a pending alert. Expects one contract publish and one migration.
- **2026-09-17 (revision 98):** Shipped `NOTIF-08`, `NOTIF-02` and `NOTIF-03`
  together and released the claim. The three had to ship as one: `NOTIF-03` is
  the only part a user can feel, and it is `S` solely because it assumes the
  delivery service exists. Four decisions are worth keeping. **One pending
  alert per session, not one per completed set** -- only the running rest
  period needs an alert, so a session holds at most one row however many sets
  it logs, and the row's earlier estimate of per-set scheduling overstated the
  cost. **A pending alert is a row, not a timer**, swept by an existing
  `@nestjs/schedule` interval and claimed with `DELETE ... RETURNING`: a
  restart between logging a set and the end of rest still delivers, and two
  instances cannot both send one alert. **A subscription is retired only on a
  definitive 404 or 410**, because anything else may succeed next time and
  deleting a real device on a rate limit would silently end its alerts.
  **Push is optional infrastructure**: without VAPID keys the server runs
  unchanged and every read reports it unavailable, so no client is prompted for
  a permission that could not be honoured. Production was in exactly that state
  until the owner set the VAPID variables on Railway, after which the whole
  chain was confirmed there: a device enabled from Settings and a real alert
  delivered to a locked screen. The `NOTIF-03` scope limit stands
  unchanged: a static line, never a ticking countdown, and on iOS only in a PWA
  installed to the Home Screen, which the Settings card checks before every
  other refusal so an iOS tab is never told to fix the server instead.
  Generating the migration also closed a latent defect: the
  `training_event_user_type_occurred` index was created by hand in the `ACH-01`
  migration and never declared in `schema.prisma`, so every migration since had
  been proposing to drop it. It is now declared, and this migration is purely
  additive.
- **2026-09-17 (revision 99):** Closed the push slice after the owner confirmed
  a real rest alert arriving on a locked screen in production. Corrected two
  rows while closing. Active-queue group 9 said only `SCHED-01–07` and
  `NOTIF-01` had shipped, which stopped being true when delivery landed; only
  `NOTIF-04` and `NOTIF-05` remain in it. And `NOTIF-04` named `PREF-04` as a
  dependency, which reads as a block on locale and formatting preferences that
  do not exist: the account already stores a time zone, and the only missing
  piece a reminder needs is quiet hours, which `NOTIF-05` owns. The two are now
  recorded as shipping together, which makes them dependency-ready.
- **2026-09-17 (revision 100):** Claimed `NOTIF-05` and `NOTIF-04` as one slice
  across contracts, backend and frontend, `NOTIF-05` first because `NOTIF-04`
  cannot respect quiet hours that do not exist yet. One decision is recorded
  before the work rather than after, because it changes what `NOTIF-04` can
  truthfully promise. **The product does not know what time anyone trains.** A
  routine day carries a weekday, never an hour, and no session stores an
  intended start, so "remind me thirty minutes before my session" has nothing
  to count back from. Inferring a usual hour from history was rejected: it
  would be a guess presented as a schedule, and it contradicts the retained
  rule that automated behaviour shows evidence and does not invent. `NOTIF-04`
  therefore reminds **at a time the owner picks, on the days they are planned
  to train** -- which is exactly what the schedule knows. The planned-day rule
  is ported to the backend over `Routine` and `ScheduleOverride` rather than
  read from the frontend's `buildScheduleWeek`, and a date that already has a
  session produces no reminder. Expects one contract publish and one migration
  generalising `ScheduledPush` beyond a session.
- **2026-09-17 (revision 101):** Shipped `NOTIF-05` and `NOTIF-04`, completing
  active-queue group 9. Four decisions are recorded because each one narrows
  what a later change may do. **A reminder is a time, not a countdown** -- the
  decision taken at claim time survived the build, and the Settings card says
  so in as many words rather than leaving the user to infer it. **Both controls
  are applied at send, in one function.** Applying them at schedule would let an
  alert set before quiet hours begin arrive inside them, and would let a
  category switched off afterwards still fire; switching a category off also
  deletes its pending rows, because a notification arriving after the switch
  reads as the control not working. **Channels and digests were not built, and
  the row said "optional digests" for digests only.** Web Push is the only
  delivery channel, so a channel switch would be the category switch again, and
  a digest of two notification types batches nothing; building either would have
  been a control that does not control anything. **Local time is only ever read
  forwards.** Mapping a chosen local minute back onto an instant fires twice or
  never on the two daylight-saving days a year, so the planner matches the
  current local minute against a catch-up window instead, and the per-date
  dedupe key keeps that window to one reminder. The width and theme sweep was
  outstanding at first close, for two reasons worth remembering: a verification
  script that opened one browser context per width rotated the saved sign-in's
  refresh token until it was invalid, and the regression project refuses to run
  while a workout session is active, which it was. Both were the owner's to
  clear; the sweep then passed in both themes at every boundary width.
- **2026-09-17 (revision 102):** Claimed `NOTIF-06` and `ROUT-04`, in that
  order, on separate branches because they share no module. Two decisions are
  recorded before the work. **`NOTIF-06` has an exact rule rather than an
  invented one.** A streak survives a gap of three days and dies on the fourth
  (`nextStreak` in `analytics-contribution.ts`), so "at risk" means the third
  day with no session yet -- the last date that can still save it. It is
  delivered at the `NOTIF-04` reminder time as its own category and payload,
  and it **replaces** that day's training reminder rather than sending a second
  push. It is evaluated whether or not the day is planned, which is what makes
  it worth having: a planned day already gets a reminder. It states the
  evidence and never instructs anyone to train, because a rest day ending a
  streak is the plan working. **`ROUT-04` shares the routine, not the
  training.** A shared routine is the prescription -- days, exercises, sets,
  rest, progression -- and never the owner's sessions, records or notes. It
  follows the shipped `SOC-07` session-share precedent for revocable
  private links, and the account-level `PROF-06` routines rule is the upper
  bound on any per-routine visibility, the same way `SOC-04` bounds activity.
  The profile showcase stays with `PROF-08` and the clone with `ROUT-05`.
  Expects one contract publish and one migration each.
- **2026-09-17 (revision 103):** Shipped `NOTIF-06`. The rule is the streak
  rule already in the code rather than a new opinion: a run survives a gap of
  three days and dies on the fourth, so the nudge fires on day three and on no
  other day. Two constraints kept it honest. **It replaces the day's training
  reminder rather than joining it**, so an account never receives two pushes
  about the same day; the shared per-date key is what makes that automatic.
  **It states dates and carries no imperative**, asserted by a test, because a
  rest day ending a streak is the plan working and telling someone to train
  through it would be the generic daily pressure this feature exists instead
  of. Evaluating it on unplanned days too is what earns it a place beside
  `NOTIF-04`, which already covers the planned ones.
- **2026-09-17 (revision 104):** Shipped `ROUT-04`, which unblocks `ROUT-05`,
  `ROUT-06`, `ROUT-07` and `PROF-08`. Three decisions are worth keeping.
  **The shared payload is `captureRoutineSetup` rather than a new projection.**
  That shape exists for `ROUT-08` versions and has nowhere to put a session, a
  record or a note, so reusing it makes leaking training a compile error rather
  than a review item; a hand-written projection would only have been correct
  until someone added a field. **The account rule caps the routine, never the
  reverse.** Both `PROF-06` routines visibility and the routine's own setting
  apply and the narrower wins, so marking one routine public inside a
  followers-only account reaches followers -- and the owner is told, because
  silently doing the safe thing would leave them believing they had published
  it. **A denied routine answers 404.** A 403 would confirm the routine exists,
  which is a different kind of disclosure from refusing to serve it. The owner
  chose to include target loads: a programme without them is not one a reader
  can follow, and a target is a plan rather than a performance. Sharing is the
  capability only -- the profile showcase stays with `PROF-08` and the clone
  with `ROUT-05`.
- **2026-09-18 (revision 105):** Claimed `ROUT-05` and `PROF-08` as one slice.
  They are claimed together rather than in sequence because both read the same
  `ROUT-04` surface -- a routine somebody else may see -- and splitting them
  would have built that reader's page twice. Four decisions are recorded before
  the work. **A clone copies the prescription and reuses the shipped
  projection.** The payload a reader already receives is `RoutineVersionSetup`,
  and `setupToRoutineUpdate` already turns one back into a routine for
  `ROUT-08` restores; the clone is those two ends joined, so a field that could
  carry the original owner's training would have to be added to a shape that
  has nowhere to put one. **A clone starts private and independent.** It is a
  new routine owned by whoever cloned it, with no versions, no links, no
  favourite or completed state and `PRIVATE` visibility -- inheriting `PUBLIC`
  would republish someone else's programme under a new name without anyone
  choosing to. Lineage stays with `ROUT-06`. **`PROF-08` is a fourth typed slot,
  not a second curation surface.** The shipped `PROF-07` model already stores an
  ordered six typed references and revalidates them at read time, so a routine
  is one more `kind` in it; a parallel showcase would have meant a second
  Settings card, a second profile section and two orderings to reconcile.
  **The narrower rule still wins, and `canViewRoutine` is the one that says so.**
  A featured routine is resolved through the same function the `ROUT-04` reads
  use, so the account-level `PROF-06` routines rule caps each routine's own
  visibility on a profile exactly as it does everywhere else. Expects one
  contract publish and one migration, for the new featured kind.
- **2026-09-18 (revision 106):** Shipped `ROUT-05` and `PROF-08` as one slice,
  which completes queue group 5 except for `PROF-10` and makes `ROUT-06` and
  `ROUT-07` dependency-ready. Four decisions are worth keeping. **A clone is
  two shipped pieces joined at the ends.** `SharedRoutine.setup` is what a
  reader already receives and `setupToRoutineUpdate` already turns one back
  into a routine for a `ROUT-08` restore, so cloning is those two and nothing
  else; writing a third projection would have created the first place where
  the original owner's training could leak, and a test asserts what the created
  payload does _not_ contain rather than only what it does. **A clone starts
  private.** The copy is independent from the moment it exists, so it takes the
  routine defaults -- no links, no versions, no favourite or completed state
  and `PRIVATE` visibility. Inheriting `PUBLIC` would have republished somebody
  else's programme under a new owner without anyone choosing to, which is a
  different act from cloning it. **The showcase is a fourth `kind`, not a
  second surface.** `PROF-07` already stores an ordered six typed references,
  revalidates them against current state and omits what a viewer may not see;
  a routine needed none of that built again, only a `SharedRoutineSummary`
  payload and one more branch. A parallel showcase would have meant a second
  Settings card, a second profile section and two orderings to reconcile on
  every future change. **The same rule, asked twice, is not the same
  question.** Selecting and reading a featured routine differ: an owner must
  not be able to feature a routine nobody else can open, because the slot would
  silently never render, while a viewer must get exactly what `canViewRoutine`
  allows. Both call the shipped `ROUT-04` function rather than re-deriving the
  rule, so the account-level `PROF-06` routines rule still caps each routine's
  own visibility everywhere. What was left unverified is recorded in the
  completion log. One decision was added by the browser pass rather than
  before it. **An empty state must name the rule that is actually binding.**
  With the account routines rule `PRIVATE` the picker correctly offered
  nothing, but it said no routine was shared yet and sent the owner to a
  per-routine control the account rule would go on capping -- the same failure
  `ROUT-04` guarded against when it decided the owner is told their routine is
  being capped rather than silently given the safe outcome. The copy now names
  the account rule first. The full `ui:regression` sweep passed 409/409 and the
  clone, the featured slot and all four narrower-wins combinations were
  exercised end to end against the running stack; what a second account would
  have added -- a follower's and a stranger's _authenticated_ view -- is the
  one gap left, and the signed-out read plus the backend tests cover the same
  branches.
- **2026-09-18 (revision 107):** Claimed `PROF-10` and `ROUT-06` as one slice.
  They share no module -- one is the block relation and every read that must
  respect it, the other is one column on a routine -- but they are claimed
  together because both are small enough to verify in one pass and neither
  blocks the other. Four decisions are recorded before the work. **A block is
  symmetric and server-enforced, not a mute.** It removes any follow in both
  directions, prevents a new one either way, and hides each account from the
  other's search, suggestions, relationship lists and profile reads; a control
  that only hid the blocked account from the blocker would leave the blocker
  visible to them, which is the half nobody asks for. **A blocked profile
  answers 404, exactly as a denied routine does under `ROUT-04`.** A 403 would
  confirm the account exists and that a block is the reason, which tells the
  blocked member something the blocker did not choose to tell them. **A block
  is not retroactive over what was already handed out.** A `SOC-07` session
  link and a `ROUT-04` routine link carry no viewer identity -- the token is
  the credential -- so a block cannot revoke them and must not pretend to;
  revoking the link is the control that does that, and the copy says so.
  **A report is recorded, not acted on.** `PROF-10` stores the report with its
  subject, reason and reporter and confirms receipt honestly; the queue, the
  review actions and the enforcement record are `TRUST-04`, and nothing here
  claims a human will read it on any timetable. **`ROUT-06` records what a
  clone came from and nothing about who may see it.** `SharedRoutine.routineId`
  already travels for this purpose; the clone stores the source routine and its
  author, and the lineage is shown only when the viewer could read the source
  anyway -- lineage must not become a way to learn that a private routine
  exists. Expects one contract publish and one migration each.
- **2026-09-18 (revision 108):** Shipped `PROF-10` and `ROUT-06`, completing
  active-queue group 5 and leaving `TRUST-04` as the single gate on the rest of
  the social core. Five decisions are worth keeping. **The block rule lives in
  pure helpers, not an injected service.** Each service calls them with its own
  `db`, so no module wiring can quietly make a privacy control fail open; the
  existing mocks were updated to say "this account blocks nobody" explicitly for
  the same reason -- a fixture that omits the block read would let a block that
  should hide something pass by silence. **Blocking removes the follows, both
  ways, in the same transaction.** Filtering them at read time would leave a
  follower count that still includes a blocked member, and would leave them
  holding whatever access followers have. **404 before any sensitive read.**
  A blocked profile is indistinguishable from one that does not exist, exactly
  as `ROUT-04` decided for a denied routine, and a test counts reads to prove
  authorization comes first. **What a block cannot do is said out loud.** A
  `SOC-07` session link and a `ROUT-04` routine link carry no viewer identity,
  so a block cannot withdraw one; both the confirmation and the Settings card
  say that revoking the link is the control for that, because a safety feature
  that quietly does less than it appears to is worse than one that states its
  limit. **A report is acknowledged as recorded and nothing more.** Nothing
  reads these rows yet, so the receipt promises no review and no timetable, and
  a test asserts the wording does not drift into one. `ROUT-06` then reuses the
  one rule the routine surfaces already share: lineage is resolved through
  `canViewRoutine`, so a clone can never become a way to learn that a private
  routine exists. The gap left is the same one `ROUT-05` left: with a single
  local account, a block between two real members was not driven through the
  browser.
- **2026-09-19 (revision 109):** Closed the two-member gap `ROUT-05` and
  `PROF-10` both left open, without creating a second account. A temporary
  member was seeded straight into the database, every block branch was
  exercised from **both** sides against real rows, and the fixture was
  deleted; 23 checks passed and no rows remained. Two things are worth
  recording. **A seeded row is not an account, and that is what made it
  usable.** It carries no `supabaseUserId` and no password, so it can never
  sign in -- it exists only as the other side of the relation, which is all
  the symmetric reads need. **The gap was smaller than it looked.** A block is
  one code path read from both directions, so verifying it with the ids
  swapped covers what a second session would have shown, and only a real
  browser view as the blocked member is still unexercised. A stray test report
  row left by the `PROF-10` browser pass was also removed, restoring the
  database to its prior state; the ad-hoc script that seeds and deletes the
  fixture was not committed, because a script that writes to the deployment
  database is not something to leave in `scripts/` for someone to run by
  accident.
- **2026-09-19 (revision 110):** Claimed `ROUT-07`. The row asks for six
  filters and the audit found that **four are derivable from data that
  already exists and two are not**, which is the decision the slice turns on.
  Days, muscle focus and equipment come from the routine's own exercises
  through the `EXER-09` catalog metadata, and duration comes from `ROUT-10`'s
  `estimateDaySeconds`, ported to the server the way `NOTIF-04` ported the
  schedule rule, with its assumptions stated wherever the number is shown.
  **Goal and experience are not on a routine and will not be guessed.** They
  exist only on `User` as `PROF-05` training identity, which describes the
  person, not the programme; attributing an author's goals to their routine
  would invent data exactly as the fabricated profile content in `FIX-01` and
  the invented completion ratio the routine card refuses to show. They become
  **optional owner-declared fields on the routine**, reusing the existing
  `TrainingGoal` and `TrainingExperienceLevel` enums rather than a parallel
  vocabulary, and a routine that declares neither is simply not matched by
  those two filters and says so rather than defaulting to something.
  **Discovery is not a new visibility tier.** It lists exactly what
  `canViewRoutine` already allows -- public routines in public accounts to
  everyone, followers-only ones to followers -- so nothing becomes findable
  that was not already readable, and `PROF-10` removes both parties of a block
  from each other's results. **The facets are derived at read time over a
  capped candidate set rather than denormalised onto the routine**, because a
  stored facet drifts from the routine the moment an edit misses its
  recompute; the cap is a contract constant and the response reports when it
  was reached, as `ACH-05` reports a truncated scan. Expects one contract
  publish and one migration for the two declared fields.
- **2026-09-19 (revision 111):** Shipped `ROUT-07`, which gives the routine
  ecosystem the front door `ROUT-04`, `ROUT-05` and `ROUT-06` had built
  machinery for and no way into. Three decisions are worth keeping. **Two of
  the six filters could not be honestly derived, so they were declared
  instead.** A routine's days, muscles, equipment and duration are facts about
  what it programs; its goal and experience level are claims, and the only
  place the product held anything like them was the author's own `PROF-05`
  training identity. Reading a person's goals onto their programme would have
  been the same class of invention as the biography `FIX-01` deleted, so the
  routine carries its own optional fields and an undeclared one answers no
  filter at all. **Derived facets are computed per read.** Storing them would
  have made discovery faster and wrong: the first routine edit that missed a
  recompute would leave a filter pointing at a routine that no longer matches
  it, and a filter that quietly disagrees with its result is worse than no
  filter. The cap and the reported truncation are the price of that choice and
  are stated rather than hidden. **Discovery revealed nothing new.** The
  candidate set is the shipped `canViewRoutine`, so the feature is a new way
  to reach routines that were already readable, and the page says exactly that
  before listing anything -- otherwise shipping a browse surface would quietly
  redefine what members thought sharing meant.
- **2026-09-19 (revision 112):** Claimed `SOC-03` and `SOC-04` as one slice.
  They ship together rather than in sequence because `SOC-03` may not display
  anything until `SOC-04`'s controls exist, so the feed alone would be a
  surface with nothing it is allowed to show. Eight decisions are recorded
  before the work. **An entry is one verified fact.** The types are the ones
  the product can already verify: completed sessions, personal records, load
  progressions (`PROGRESSION_CHANGED`), earned achievements, streak milestones,
  `ACH-05` comebacks and `ROUT-04` shared routines. A session with three
  records is three entries, shown together, so each can be overridden on its
  own. A streak milestone is written as both an `ACHIEVEMENT_UNLOCKED` and a
  `STREAK_MILESTONE` event; it is reported once, as the streak. Achievements
  recognized from history never produce an entry, exactly as `NOTIF-01`
  decided, because they have no honest date. **Rank progression is not an
  activity type yet.** Nothing records when a rank was reached, and deriving
  the moment would replay a member's whole attendance history on every read,
  which the product rules forbid; it waits on a writer that records it, the way
  `ACH-01` records milestones. Challenges are `SOC-13`, unshipped, and are not
  stubbed. **Entries are generated on read and never stored.** They come from
  `TrainingEvent` rows, the shipped `comebackRecognitionSummary` with its own
  bounds, and the routine; only the owner's choices are stored -- a default
  audience per type and an override per entry, keyed by the entry's stable
  source key -- for the reason `ROUT-07` gave: a stored copy drifts from what
  it copies. A shared routine needs the one fact that does not exist,
  **when** it was shared, so `Routine.sharedAt` is set when a routine first
  becomes visible to anyone; routines shared before this ships have none and
  produce no entry rather than an invented date. **Every type starts at Only
  me.** Visibility is never implicit: nothing reaches anyone until its owner
  picks an audience for that type, from the `PROF-06` values, and the control
  says that a default applies to past entries of the type as well as future
  ones. **Profile privacy is the upper bound, through the shipped rule.** Each
  type maps to one section -- sessions and load progressions to workout
  history, records to records, achievements, streaks and comebacks to
  achievements, shared routines to routines -- and an entry is visible only
  when `canViewProfileSection` allows both the section and the entry's
  audience, the shape `canViewRoutine` already has; a shared routine must also
  pass `canViewRoutine` itself. An override may widen or narrow one entry but
  never past its section, and the owner is told when a section is capping a
  choice, as `ROUT-04` tells them about a capped routine. **Withdrawing is an
  override to Only me**, so an entry has one state rather than two that could
  disagree. **Who sees it, and where.** The feed at `/activity` shows the
  members the viewer follows; a member's profile shows their activity to
  whoever its audience allows, so Everyone and Followers differ; Everyone
  means every signed-in member, and activity stays off the signed-out
  `/members` page. The preview runs the same read as a hypothetical follower
  or non-follower, so what the owner is shown is what that audience receives
  rather than a second calculation of it. **Links are decided by the server.**
  An entry always names its record and links to it only where the viewer may
  open it: the owner reaches their own pages; another member reaches a record
  only while it is still the current best the profile shows, an achievement,
  streak or comeback through the profile ledger, and a routine through the
  member routine page; a session or a load progression has no page another
  member may open, so it carries no link. `PROF-10` applies through the pure
  helpers with the service's own `db`: blocked members never appear in each
  other's feed, and a member's activity answers 404 across a block. Expects
  one contract publish and one migration.
- **2026-09-20 (revision 113):** Shipped `SOC-03` and `SOC-04`, the verified
  activity the rest of the social core hangs from; `SOC-05` and `SOC-08` are
  now dependency-ready and `SOC-06` still waits on `TRUST-04`. The eight
  decisions of revision 112 held, and four are worth keeping in the form they
  took. **The section rule was reused, not restated, and a test proves it is
  the narrower of two.** `canViewActivity` is `resolveProfileViewerAccess` on
  the type's section plus `canViewProfileSection` on the entry's audience,
  and a test runs every type through every section rule, audience and viewer
  and compares it with the rule applied to the narrower of the two; the
  shared-routine query asks `canViewRoutine` about each value a routine can
  hold instead of copying its logic into a `where`. A private records section
  therefore cannot produce a public record entry by default or by override,
  and the real-stack pass watched exactly that happen. **The preview is the
  read.** Previewing an audience runs the feed's own code path as a
  hypothetical follower or non-follower, so the owner is shown what that
  audience receives, links included, rather than a client-side reconstruction
  that could disagree with it; the real-stack pass compared the preview with
  what the second member actually read and found them equal. **Ties are the
  normal case, so the cursor does not compare keys.** A workout's facts share
  its end time; the cursor carries that instant and short digests of what was
  already returned at it, which keeps paging correct whatever the database's
  collation does with a key, and keeps the cursor short enough for a URL.
  **Withdrawing is one state, not two.** Choosing Only me for an entry is the
  withdrawal, so a later change to the type's default can never quietly
  republish something its owner took down. One decision was added by the
  work rather than before it: routines shared before this shipped have no
  share date and produce no entry, the same honesty `NOTIF-01` applied to
  achievements recognized from history; none existed locally, so it cost
  nothing here, but a deployment with shared routines will show none of them
  in activity until they are shared again. Declaring three `Routine` indexes that earlier migrations had
  created by hand also ended a schema drift that made every generated
  migration propose dropping them. What was left unverified is in the
  completion log.
- **2026-09-20 (revision 115):** Shipped `SOC-05`, the first thing that
  reads `SOC-03` activity rather than producing it, and the seven decisions of
  revision 114 held. Three are worth keeping. **The gate was inherited, not
  rebuilt.** A reaction summary is a field of the entry, and the write path
  resolves the entry's author and re-runs the same plan, so there is no second
  answer to "may this viewer see this entry" that could drift from the first;
  a 404 rather than a 403 keeps an entry the viewer may not see
  indistinguishable from one that does not exist, as `ROUT-04` and `PROF-10`
  both decided. **A count is arithmetic, and arithmetic leaks.** Members
  either side of a block are filtered out of the counts, because a number that
  included somebody the viewer blocked would disclose them without naming
  them; for the same reason no list of who reacted ships, since that is an
  identity surface with its own privacy question rather than a detail of this
  one. **Nothing else may read these rows.** They feed no total, no rank and
  no ordering, and one row per member per entry means there is nothing to
  accumulate -- the guard that keeps an acknowledgement from becoming a score.
  One thing was added by the browser pass rather than before it: four
  unlabelled classical glyphs said nothing to a pointer, so each carries a
  tooltip beside the accessible name it already had, and the pressed state and
  the count still move independently of both.
- **2026-09-20 (revision 114):** Claimed `SOC-05`. It follows `SOC-03`/`SOC-04`
  directly: a reaction is an acknowledgement of one generated activity entry,
  so it inherits a visibility model that already exists rather than needing
  one. Seven decisions are recorded before the work. **The activity read is
  the only gate.** A summary travels inside the entry it belongs to, so
  nothing can display a reaction on an entry the viewer was not allowed to
  see, and accepting one resolves the entry's author and re-runs the same
  plan, refusing with 404 when the viewer may not read it -- there is no
  endpoint that would answer questions about an entry they cannot see.
  **The catalog is four, fixed in the contract, and themed**: Strength,
  Discipline, Respect and Inspiring, each with one of the classical icons the
  app already ships. They acknowledge work: nothing sorts by them, no total
  counts them, and they touch no rank, record or achievement. **One per
  member per entry** -- choosing another replaces it and choosing the same
  one again removes it -- so there is nothing to accumulate. **Aggregates
  only, no reactor list.** Who reacted is an identity surface with its own
  privacy question, and a name beside an entry would say more about the
  reactor than about the work; this slice ships counts and the viewer's own
  choice. **A block removes both directions from the count**, because a
  number that included somebody the viewer blocked would leak them through
  arithmetic. **Nobody reacts to their own entry**, which is refused rather
  than hidden: acknowledging your own work is not what the feature is for.
  **No notification in this slice**; the owner sees reactions on their own
  entries in Activity › Yours, and `NOTIF-01` gathers from sources that are
  training events, which a reaction is not. Expects one contract publish and
  one migration.
- **2026-09-20 (revision 116):** Claimed `TRUST-04`. `PROF-10` shipped the
  member-facing half on 2026-09-18 and `MemberReport` rows have been
  accumulating with nothing that reads them; `SOC-06` and every `COMM-*` item
  wait on the review side. Nine decisions are recorded before the work.
  **A moderator is a flag on the account, not a role system.** There is one
  moderator, the owner, so `User.isModerator` is the whole authorization
  model and a seed sets it for that account alone; a roles-and-permissions
  table would be a subsystem built for a second moderator who does not exist.
  **Two review actions, and no third.** A report may be **dismissed**, or the
  reported subject **hidden from everyone but its owner**. Nothing in this
  slice deletes a routine, a session or an account: a member's training data
  is theirs, and a moderation decision is not a reason to destroy it.
  **Hiding reuses the shipped rule rather than adding a gate beside it.** A
  hidden routine is refused by `canViewRoutine` before it asks anything else,
  a hidden session share stops resolving for its token, and a hidden account
  answers 404 and drops out of search, suggestions, relationship lists,
  activity and discovery -- the same shape `PROF-10` already gives a block.
  Every one of those exclusions goes through one pair of helpers, so a read
  added later cannot quietly omit it. **The owner is told.** A routine or a
  share that has stopped resolving while its own control still reads "Public"
  would be a switch with nothing behind it, which the product rules forbid, so
  the owner's routine and share surfaces state that moderation has hidden it.
  **Reviewing grants no new sight.** The queue resolves each subject through
  the shipped `PROF-06`/`PROF-10` rules **as the reviewer**, and says plainly
  when a subject is withheld from them rather than revealing it; there is no
  moderator bypass, and a reviewer opens a subject on its own page rather than
  through a mirror of it that could answer differently. A moderator who must
  read private content has to be granted it the way anyone else would.
  **Reading is itself an action.** Opening a reported subject writes a
  `VIEW_SUBJECT` record with the moderator and the time, for the same reason
  the enforcement records exist: an unlogged read is the one moderation power
  nobody could ever audit. **Enforcement records are append-only.** A
  `ModerationAction` row is never updated and never deleted; the service has
  no path that could. **A hide is undone by appending, not by editing.**
  Restoring writes a new `RESTORE_SUBJECT` record that supersedes the hide,
  which is the only way "never editable" and "a mistaken hide is not
  permanent" can both hold. This is one action more than the two named above
  and is deliberately not a third enforcement power -- it removes one.
  **The receipt does not change.** `PROF-10`'s report copy says the report was
  recorded, that reviews happen as moderation is built out and that the
  reporter will get no update on it. A queue existing is not a timetable, and
  the copy must not drift into one now that somebody can read the row.
  Expects one contract publish and one migration.
- **2026-09-21 (revision 117):** Shipped `TRUST-04`, the review side of
  `PROF-10` and the last gate on queue group 8; `SOC-06` is now
  dependency-ready, and every `COMM-*` item has one fewer unmet dependency.
  The nine decisions of revision 116 held. Five are worth keeping in the form
  they took. **The absence of a bypass is the feature, and it was verified by
  its consequence.** The queue resolves each reported subject through the
  shipped `PROF-06`/`PROF-10` rules with the moderator as the viewer, so a
  subject they may not read comes back withheld with a null title and a null
  owner and no link. The real-stack pass watched the consequence land:
  **after hiding a routine, the moderator who hid it could no longer read it
  either.** That is correct rather than awkward -- the enforcement record, not
  a privileged read, is how a decision is revisited -- and it is the property
  that keeps one boolean from being a key to every private profile in the
  product. **Reading is an action, so it is recorded before it happens.**
  Opening a subject writes a `VIEW_SUBJECT` row and the reviewer then opens
  the subject's own page; there is no moderation mirror of the content,
  because a second read path is a second answer to "may this viewer see this",
  and the one that drifts is always the one nobody is looking at. **Hiding
  narrows the shipped rule rather than standing beside it.** The required
  `moderation` argument added to `canViewRoutine` is what made the compiler
  name every read that had to consider a hide; the same instinct replaced five
  duplicated block lookups with one pair of helpers that answer blocks and
  hides together, so a read added later cannot honour one and omit the other.
  **A hide takes nothing away from the person it is about.** The routine keeps
  its days, versions and links, the share keeps its token with `revokedAt`
  still null, the account keeps everything it owns and still reads its own
  profile -- and the owner is told, because a routine that resolves for nobody
  while its own control still reads "Public" is a switch with nothing behind
  it. **Undoing a hide appends rather than edits**, which is the only way an
  uneditable record and a reversible mistake can both be true; that restore is
  one action more than the two the roadmap row named, and it removes a power
  rather than adding one. Two things were added by the work rather than before
  it. The browser pass found the withheld note ending "You can still dismiss
  the report or hide the content" on a row that was showing only Restore, so
  the note is now two constants and the second is appended only when one of
  those actions is on offer. A second read of the diff caught the same class
  of drift in a label: `ACTIONED` read "Content hidden", which is a claim
  about the subject rather than about the report, and two reports can name one
  subject -- restoring through one would leave the other saying "Content
  hidden" over content that is visible. **A status describes what was decided
  about the report; `subject.isHidden` is the live fact**, and the row already
  shows it separately, so the label is now "Acted on". And the local stack held **no reports at all**:
  the premise that rows had been accumulating since `PROF-10` is true of
  production, not of the restored development database, so the queue was
  exercised entirely against a seeded fixture. Two runs of the sweep were
  discarded before the one that counts -- a backend that went down mid-run,
  then a lapsed sign-in that `ui:refresh` could not renew -- and neither was
  reported as a result. What was left unverified is in the completion log.
- **2026-09-21 (revision 118):** Claimed `SOC-06`. It was the last item
  blocked on moderation, and `TRUST-04` closed that gate the same day. Ten
  decisions are recorded before the work. **A comment is the first free text
  one member writes for another to read**, which is what separates this from
  everything shipped so far: activity entries are derived from verified
  training, reactions are a fixed catalogue of four, and a `PROF-10` report
  goes only to the moderator. Every decision below follows from that.
  **A comment hangs off an activity entry key, not a new object.** `SOC-03`
  entries are generated on read and never stored, so the anchor is the stable
  `entryKey` `SOC-05` already keys reactions by — an event's `eventKey`, a
  `comeback:<id>` or a `routine:<id>` — with the author resolved and stored
  beside it the same way. Nothing new identifies an activity. **The activity
  read is the only gate, reused rather than restated.** A comment list travels
  inside the entry it belongs to, so a comment can never render on an entry
  the viewer was not allowed to see; posting resolves the entry's author,
  rebuilds that viewer's plan and answers **404** when it does not allow the
  entry, never 403, which would confirm it exists. **Anyone who may read the
  entry may comment on it** (decided with the owner): no second visibility
  rule, because a separate permission is the thing `SOC-05` and `TRUST-04`
  both deliberately avoided, and an audience of Everyone already means every
  signed-in member. **Two people may delete a comment**: its author, and the
  owner of the activity it hangs from. The owner's reach is the point — it is
  their workout the comment is attached to — and a deletion is a real delete
  rather than a flag, because nothing else reads these rows. **A comment is
  reportable, which is what makes it the first real use of the queue.**
  `ReportSubjectKind` gains `COMMENT`, and `TRUST-04`'s resolver gains one
  branch; a moderator hide narrows the comment exactly as it narrows a routine
  — gone for everyone but its author, deleted for nobody. **Blocks apply in
  both directions**, as they do to reaction counts, because a comment from
  somebody the viewer blocked is worse than a number that included them.
  **The owner is notified** (decided with the owner): `NOTIF-01` gathers on
  read from training events, and a comment is a row rather than an event, so
  this is a genuinely new source shape rather than another branch of the same
  one — the first thing in the notification centre that another member caused
  on purpose. **Flat, bounded and plain.** No threads, no mentions, no
  editing, no reactions on comments, no comment timeline and no per-member
  comment page; a length cap and a per-day rate limit, both contract
  constants, and the count travels with the entry so a feed page stays one
  query. **Nothing else may read these rows** — no total, no rank, no
  ordering input — the guard that keeps acknowledgement from becoming a score,
  carried over from `SOC-05`. Expects one contract publish and one migration.
- **2026-09-22 (revision 119):** Shipped `SOC-06`, and with it the first free
  text one member writes for another to read. The ten decisions of revision
  118 held. Four are worth keeping in the form they took. **The gate was
  inherited twice over.** A comment hangs off the stable activity key `SOC-05`
  already keys reactions by, and `resolveCommentableEntry` runs the same
  resolution a reaction goes through, so reading, writing and deleting all
  answer 404 for an entry the viewer may not see -- there is no second answer
  to "may this viewer see this entry" that could drift from the first. The one
  deliberate difference from a reaction is that the owner is allowed through:
  replying on your own activity is ordinary, where acknowledging your own work
  is not. **The count and the list are one answer.** Both sides of a block and
  every moderator-hidden comment are excluded from each, so a number can never
  advertise a comment the viewer cannot open; the real-stack pass watched a
  block empty both and an unblock restore both. **Deleting belongs to two
  people and not to a moderator.** The author and the owner of the activity
  may each remove a comment, because an entry its owner cannot clear would be
  somebody else's surface on their workout; `TRUST-04` hides instead, which
  keeps the enforcement record the only account of what a moderator did.
  **A notification names a comment rather than quoting it.** The row outlives
  what it describes and three different people can remove that text, so a
  copy in the notification centre could be withdrawn by none of them. Two
  things were added by the work rather than before it. The real-stack pass
  found that `assertSubjectExists` picked its table with a chain of ternaries
  whose last branch was unnamed, so a `COMMENT` report fell through to the
  session-share lookup and answered 404 -- no pure test covered it, and it is
  a switch over the union now so the compiler names the next kind added. And
  the delete confirmation was a native `window.confirm`, which appears nowhere
  else in the product; it is an `AlertDialog` like every other destructive
  confirmation, and it says which of the two deletions the viewer is making.
  What was left unverified is in the completion log.

- **2026-09-22 (revision 120):** Resolved `LIVE-06`, which had been `BLOCKED`
  on a product decision since 2026-09-07, and claimed it. Three decisions, the
  first of which shrank the item from `L` to `S`.

  **The capability already exists; only the route to it was missing.**
  `startSession` validates ownership of the routine day and nothing about the
  date, and `/routines/[id]` already passes a specific `dayId`, so training a
  different day today works end to end today -- it is just four taps deep,
  behind Routines and a routine. `LIVE-06` is therefore surfacing it at the
  point of need, not building it. What Quick Workout did was different and
  simply broken: it created a routine whose only day had `exercises: []`, so
  the session had nothing loggable, which is why `FIX-04` hid it.

  **The template direction is rejected for this item.** Picking a curated
  programme answers "I have no routine at all", which is onboarding and is
  already `ROUT-03`'s. Adopting it here would make a two-tap fix wait on
  authoring a programme library that still has no owning ID. The two stay
  separate, and the gap for a brand-new user stays visible under `ROUT-03`
  rather than being closed by widening this item.

  **Free-form ad-hoc sessions are rejected outright**, not deferred. Everything
  in the system attributes through the routine snapshot -- progression,
  records, analytics, activity -- and a session whose prescription changes
  mid-flight is a second answer to "what was prescribed", which this codebase
  refuses elsewhere on purpose: `LIVE-11` keeps the snapshot as the
  prescription even when an exercise is substituted. It is also exactly what
  made the original session unloggable. Unstructured logging, if it is ever
  wanted, is its own decision with its own progression and attribution rules.

- **2026-09-22 (revision 121):** Shipped `SOC-08` and released its claim.
  Training partners are a separate, mutual relationship rather than a stronger
  follow. A pending request grants nothing; after acceptance each member owns
  independent schedule, progress, activity, routine and encouragement flags for
  the other. The first four only widen the matching follower-context read, and
  schedule returns seven dated facts without exposing prescriptions. The stored
  encouragement flag deliberately ships before its sender so `SOC-09` can reuse
  one recipient control instead of inventing a second privacy model. Blocking or
  ending removes both directions atomically. `@sunsteel/contracts@0.57.0`, the
  migration and both applications passed local gates, GitHub CI and deployment;
  the full UI sweep passed 452/452. The two-account browser gaps are recorded in
  the completion log and closure record. `SOC-09` is now dependency-ready.
- **2026-09-22 (revision 122):** Claimed `SOC-09` for Codex after `SOC-08`
  deployed. The slice reuses the active-partnership check and the recipient's
  stored encouragement grant, sends only the four fixed contract prompts, and
  writes through the existing notification centre. It adds no free text, thread
  or conversation model. `LIVE-06` remains concurrently claimed by Claude in
  separate frontend modules.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 123):** Shipped `SOC-09` and released its claim. The
  recipient's existing training-partner grant is the only opt-in; the sender
  chooses one of four contract-owned prompts and cannot attach text. The
  rolling pair limit is enforced in a serializable transaction, which the
  real-stack race proved by accepting four of eight simultaneous writers and
  refusing the rest. Notifications reuse the existing centre and carry actor
  plus prompt kind only. Contracts `0.58.0`, all repository gates and CI,
  Railway, Vercel, the 36-case UI scope and the 17-frame portfolio passed. The
  remaining production/timing gaps are recorded in the completion log.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 124):** Decomposed the former `ROUT-09` XL into an
  ordered programme without claiming implementation: `ROUT-09` authors dated,
  immutable block setups; `ROUT-15` makes one resolver project the active block
  into schedule, dashboard and session snapshots; `ROUT-16` adds an explicit,
  bounded deload override without diagnosing recovery or mutating the routine.
  `INTEL-02` remains the separate, optional suggestion layer. The first slice
  is now the recommended next task because `ROUT-08`, `SCHED-01` and `ROUT-11`
  have already shipped.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 125):** Claimed `ROUT-09` for Codex. The implementation
  is limited to authoring and reviewing named, non-overlapping calendar blocks
  whose prescription is an immutable `RoutineVersionSetup` captured from the
  current routine or a saved `ROUT-08` version. It adds no schedule/session
  activation; that remains `ROUT-15`. Work runs in isolated CT/BE/FE worktrees
  because concurrent `TD-43` documentation changes exist in the normal checkout.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 126):** Shipped `TRUST-01` and released its claim.
  Decisions taken for the owner, who delegated them: deletion is immediate
  with no grace period (a second "deleted but not yet" state would reach every
  read); everything the member owns cascades, including comments and reactions
  on other members' activity; a moderator account is refused rather than
  cascading the append-only moderation record away; reports the member filed
  go with the account while reports about the member stay; and data export
  stays `EXPORT-01`, stated in the confirmation rather than bundled in. With
  `TD-43`'s local verification a deleted member's unexpired token would have
  recreated an empty account, so the guard now confirms with Supabase Auth on
  a lookup miss only.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 127):** Fixed `PROF-01`'s avatar upload, which had
  never worked in production because the Supabase project had no storage
  bucket. The bucket and its policies are now the project's first recorded
  migration; files live in a folder named after the Supabase auth uid, since
  that is the only identity storage RLS can see, and `TRUST-01`'s deletion
  removes that folder. `PROF-01` stays `SHIPPED`; the evidence now says what
  was broken and when it was fixed.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 128):** Shipped `EXPORT-01` and released its claim.
  Decisions: one versioned JSON document rather than several formats, reusing
  the shapes the app already serves so the file cannot describe a workout
  differently from the screen; weights stay kilograms; other members by
  username only; credentials and derived data left out and listed in the file.
  The `TRUST-01` deletion dialog now points to the export instead of saying
  none exists.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 129):** Shipped `DASH-08` and released its claim. It
  is a placement, not a feed: the dashboard reuses `useActivityFeed`, the same
  query key and cache as `/activity`, so the server's `SOC-04` audiences are the
  only thing that decides what appears and there is no dashboard read to keep
  in step with the feed. Decisions: it sits last, below `DASH-07` and
  `DASH-09`, so no training action moves; three rows of at most two facts,
  because one workout can carry a session and several records; no reactions
  or comments on the dashboard, which would turn a preview into a second place
  to hold a conversation; and the feed's empty copy rather than new copy, so
  the two surfaces cannot disagree about why nothing is there. The portfolio
  seed sets no activity sharing, so a capture of this section shows its empty
  state.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 130):** Housekeeping, no status change. Queue group 3
  still read as if `LIVE-06` were in progress; it shipped on 2026-09-22, so the
  group is recorded complete. The portfolio seed now gives each profiled peer
  `SOC-04` sharing defaults through the real service (BE `5b26c64`), so the
  Activity feed and the `DASH-08` dashboard preview have real rows in a local
  capture; the note in revision 129 that the section would show its empty
  state no longer holds. The seed's verification fails if the owner's feed is
  empty.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 131):** Shipped `LIVE-17` and released its claim.
  Decisions taken for the owner, who delegated them. **Only the latest
  completed workout, for 48 hours and until another starts**: the next
  workout is built on this one -- its "last time", the loads it prescribes and
  the records it is measured against -- so correcting an older one would mean
  replaying history, while correcting the latest lets its contribution be
  replaced in place, exactly. **Everything it fed is re-derived rather than
  annotated**: a record that a typo created and nobody beat would otherwise
  stay the owner's best forever. This amends the "immutable training events"
  rule of `DATA-02` for that one workout, and only through this service: a
  record, load-change or milestone event it made is rewritten with its
  corrected numbers under the same key, or deleted when it no longer holds.
  **Activity on a withdrawn fact goes with it**, reactions and comments
  included, because they acknowledged something that did not happen; the
  confirmation says so before saving. **A hand edit wins**: the routine's
  load change is re-derived only while its sets still read what the finish
  wrote. **The trail is append-only** and at most ten corrections per workout.
  **Only logged sets**: a planned set never touched has nothing to correct,
  and adding or removing sets stays out, as it does in a live session
  (`LIVE-11`). The size moves from `M` to `L` under the structural rule: it
  reaches contracts, a migration, a new service and the frontend.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 132):** Shipped `LIVE-16` and released its claim.
  Decisions taken for the owner. **Notes belong to the session**: the one note
  control the session screen had wrote the routine's exercise note, which is
  the standing instruction, so it is now read-only there and a separate note
  per exercise lives on the session (a JSON column beside
  `exerciseSubstitutions`, keyed by slot like it). **No window**: unlike a
  `LIVE-17` correction a note feeds no record, total or load, so it can be
  written or changed from history at any time; only a discarded workout
  refuses one. **Shared only with notes**: a shared link carries exercise
  notes under the existing `notes` field rather than a new one, so an owner's
  earlier choice to share notes or not keeps meaning the same thing. The
  backend route that edits a routine exercise's note is left in place; the
  frontend no longer calls it.
- **2026-09-23 (revision 133):** `TD-44` implemented: the service worker is
  generated at build time by `@serwist/next` from `worker/sw.ts` with a
